### Assistant
The `run_tests` budget resets in a new session — following the operating rules, `sync_deps` + a full `run_tests` became my first action (before reading anything). Real current state: **65 passed, 1 failed, 1 skipped** (66 collected) — a big jump from the 31F/12E baseline, and the Host-port + lifespan fixes worked. One failure remains, a *new* root cause:

- `test_tool_call_revoked_session_is_denied`: status 200 ✓, but `response.json()["result"]` has **no `is_error` key** → `KeyError: 'is_error'`.

From the traceback onward I read: the modern-era transport file (`_streamable_http_modern.py`), the POC's `resolver.py`/`vault.py`, and the fake in `conftest.py`. What I could confirm from those: the fake DELETE is sound (shared in-memory store), and the resolver should indeed raise `NoTokenStoredError`. And I found the serialization rule: `_to_jsonrpc_response` wraps *any* handler exception as a **JSON-RPC `error`** object, and `_write` serializes with **`exclude_none=True`** — so a `CallToolResult` with `is_error=None` (the success shape) *omits* that key. This fits the failure perfectly, and it also fits a specific POC bug: `decode_or_none` in `jwt_service.py` treats an *expired* token as invalid (the `exp` check is before the try). The `jwt_issuer` fixture has `ttl_seconds=600`, while the token verification path uses the *default* — if the default is shorter, the stored token is already expired by the time the tool runs → the resolver raises `InvalidStoredTokenError` ("The stored session for this user is no longer valid.") — a **tool-level denial, but a different message than what the test is asserting**.

To find out which of these two is actually true, I'll use `run_tests`'s `pytest_args` to run the *existing* test unmodified against a live server (`scripts/dev_server.py` binds to 127.0.0.1:8200 — which matches the security settings):

agent adapts to Pythonic code | Common core of experience; user reviews/learns from the mapping |\n| 2026-07-09 | Created AGENTS.md to maintain working context | Starting the POC from an empty repo |\n\n## Design — 10k-foot view\n\nThe vulnerability: in MCP-mediated tool calls, credentials can end up in the LLM's context and be surfaced (tool output, reasoning, logs). The design answer: **the token never enters the LLM's world; a user identifier does.**\n\nFlow (user's framing, 2026-07-09):\n\n1. The user authenticates to **our own service** (the security app).\n2. The service issues a **JWT** for the user.\n3. The user's **UUID and the JWT go into a vault** — the token is stored, not circulated.\n4. From that point on, only the **bare user identifier** is passed around (e.g. into MCP tool-call context).\n5. When a tool actually needs access, a **separate process — the security app, not the tool** — receives the user id, retrieves the token from the vault, retrieves roles, determines what the requested resource allows, and returns **only the applicable roles/capabilities** back to the tool.\n\n**Enforcement principle (inherited from ASR-002):** authorization must gate what the backend *retrieves* (scoped queries), not filter results after the fact based on client-held role information.\n\n**Why statelessness fits:** the adopted MCP mechanism (ASR-003) is Anthropic's native Claude API connector — stateless, no server-side session, per-call authorization only. A stable bare user id as the per-call credential is exactly what a stateless design requires; the vault + separate resolver process supplies the state that the connector can't hold.\n\n## Threat Model — Concrete Credential-Surfacing Paths\n\n(2026-08-18) Names the specific paths \"credentials surfaced\" covers — the gap the ecosystem check flagged (its sources named the exposure-*class*, not this design's specific paths). Each path pairs with the design requirement that defends against it; several of these requirements are now committed in Key Decisions above.\n\n**Assets to protect:** the JWT signing secret; the per-user vault token (the post-auth JWT stored against the user's UUID); any downstream credential the resolver derives on the user's behalf (a scoped DB connection, a third-party API key).\n\n**Trust boundary:** the LLM's context window — conversation history, tool-call arguments, tool-call results, anything that ends up prompt-cached or in a stored transcript — must never contain an asset above. Everything server-side of an MCP tool call (resolver, vault, database) is trusted; anything that crosses into a message Claude reads is not.\n\n| # | Exposure path | Concrete scenario | Defense |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Tool-result echo | A tool handler bug (or a debug/verbose mode left on) returns the resolved token instead of derived roles/capabilities | `IAuthorizationProvider`'s return type is a typed roles/capabilities model (Pydantic/dataclass) with no token-shaped field — not a dict that could accidentally carry one |\n| 2 | Tool-call argument | A tool signature includes a `user_id` (or worse, a credential) parameter the model fills in — model-visible and model-settable | Don't pass user id as a tool argument at all — resolve it server-side per request from the verified access token before the tool body runs (committed above) |\n| 3 | Confused deputy via #2 | If #2 isn't fixed, a prompt-injected instruction (from blog/document content the model is reasoning over) tricks the model into passing a *different* user id, and the resolver serves another user's data | Same fix as #2 — this is the sharper reason to close it, not just a context-window nicety |\n| 4 | Error/exception bubbling | A vault-lookup or resolver exception's message/stack trace carries token material and becomes (or is embedded in) the tool's error response, which MCP returns as model-readable content | Resolver/vault code catches its own exceptions and translates to a generic, tokenless error before it can become a tool result — never let a raw exception cross that boundary |\n| 5 | Logging | Request/response logging (FastAPI, MCP server, connector-facing endpoint) logs `authorization_token` or vault contents; those logs later surface back to Claude via a log-search or debugging tool | Log user id, never token values — redact `authorization_token` and any vault-returned field at the logging layer itself, not by convention at each call site |\n| 6 | Prompt injection / tool poisoning | Content the model reasons over (a subscribed blog post, an uploaded document) contains an injected instruction trying to get a tool to dump credentials, or to misuse a legitimate tool's parameters | OWASP MCP standard mitigation: narrow tool schemas (no free-text \"run this query\" shape) — and #1–#4 mean there's nothing credential-shaped for an injected instruction to successfully request even if it tries |\n| 7 | Connector-level token visibility | Whether `mcp_servers[].authorization_token` (an API *request* parameter per Anthropic's docs, not a `messages` content block) ever surfaces in the model's context or a stored transcript | Documented as outside the content window by the API's shape — but **verify empirically once the POC has a running connector call**: inspect an actual transcript/prompt-cache entry, don't take the docs' word alone |\n| 8 | State-handle hijacking (MCP spec's term) | MCP is stateless, no protocol-level session; if the resolver's roles/capabilities output were treated as proof of identity, it could be replayed outside the request it was issued for | Bind every resolver output to that request's verified user id server-side; never accept a roles/capabilities value handed back as identity proof — only the verified access token counts |\n| 9 | Persistence / blast radius | If any of #1–#8 leaks a token, it now also lives in whatever stores the conversation (client-side chat history in the researcher/writer UI, prompt cache), extending exposure past the single request | A consequence of getting #1–#8 right, not a separate control — named so it's clear \"don't log it\" alone isn't sufficient once transcripts are persisted |\n\n**Resolves the open question \"how does the user id reach the tool\":** it doesn't reach the tool as a value at all — it's resolved server-side, per request, from the verified access token, invisible to the model. See the corresponding Key Decision above.\n\n## Integration target: `ai-research-blog`\n\n- **Repo:** `/Users/billkratochvil/Dev/repos/ai-research-blog` (`BillKrat/ai-research-blog`) — AI-assisted multi-tenant blog platform: Next.js UI, Python/FastAPI API, PostgreSQL triple store, deployed on Railway (blogresearch.net).\n- **Architecture:** MVPVM pattern (ADR-0002) adapted for request-scoped HTTP; two packages split by reusability — `shared/` (reusable framework) vs `blogresearch/` (app-specific, ADR-0009); small registry-based composition root; **no DI framework, no ORM**.\n- **Most relevant docs — read before designing against this POC:**\n  - `docs/ASR.md` → **ASR-002** (security as the cross-cutting foundation; JWT-based identity; token-in-vault; bare user id across process boundaries) and **ASR-003** (MCP as the tool-layer gateway: one Python MCP server, multiple tools, native Claude API connector, OAuth 2.1 / RFC 8707 for client↔server auth).\n  - `docs/adr/0005-identity-data-in-triple-store.md` — identity/role/group/feature data lives in the **same triple store** as form data (point lookups are fine for triples), accessed via `IUserProvider`; the token-vault resolution is `IAuthorizationProvider`, a deliberately separate service-layer interface.\n  - `docs/adr/0003-provider-interface-split.md` — the one-responsibility-per-interface discipline this codebase follows.\n- **Planned interfaces to align with:** `IUserProvider` (data: role/group/feature triples for a user) and `IAuthorizationProvider` (service: takes user id + resource, resolves credentials, returns roles/capabilities). That codebase keeps the C#-style `I` prefix on interface names — match it so the port is frictionless.\n- **Inherited working style** (from that repo's AGENTS.md): user is experienced in .NET/C#, learning Python; requests arrive in .NET terms but the goal is idiomatic Python. Don't import .NET ceremony — no interface for a single implementation with no near-term second one, no deep DAL/BLL/Repository stacks for something small, no frameworks the task doesn't need.\n- **Inherited \"what to avoid\":** no DI framework/ORM/heavy dependencies; no import-time side effects outside explicit entrypoints; no app code aware of the test framework; **never hardcode credentials or connection strings** — absolute here, this *is* the security system.\n- **Extraction note:** when code leaves the POC for `ai-research-blog`, it will follow that repo's `shared/` vs `blogresearch/` boundary and ADR conventions. In the POC itself, keep things simple and self-contained — don't pre-apply that split prematurely, but do keep module boundaries clean enough that extraction is a move, not a rewrite.\n\n## Open Questions (the design work this POC exists to do)\n\nResolved 2026-08-18 (see Key Decisions and Threat Model above) — kept here as a strikethrough record so the earlier framing isn't lost: JWT shape, vault implementation, identity anchor shape, threat model, how the user id reaches the tool. Deployment topology has a recommendation logged but is not yet confirmed by the user.\n\nStill genuinely open:\n\n- **Deployment topology — confirm or override:** the Key Decisions table above recommends an in-process resolver as the starting point (fewer deployables, revisit if a real isolation/scaling reason shows up). This is a real security-boundary trade-off (a separate deployable shrinks blast radius if the MCP server is compromised), so it's logged as a recommendation, not a committed decision, pending the user weighing in. Tied to ASR-003's identical question for the MCP server's own placement — decide both together. Decision brief with the A/B/C trade-off table is `docs/0001` (pending user sign-off).\n- **Token lifecycle:** expiry/refresh strategy; whether revocation-before-expiry matters (plain JWTs can't be revoked early without a blocklist).\n- **Roles/claims model — the specific list:** the *shape* (fixed anchor + triples) is committed; the actual roles (owner, subscriber, admin?) and how they map onto blog accounts vs. Postgres data vs. Claude-mediated data access are not yet enumerated. Drafted as a decision brief in `docs/0002` (pending review).\n- **POC scope line:** which parts of the flow do we prove here (auth endpoint, vault store, resolver service, one stub tool consuming the capability result) vs. which belong to the main project from the start.\n- **Security app's API contract:** what does the resolver process actually expose — endpoint shape, request/response schema for `IAuthorizationProvider`. Not yet defined — drafted as `docs/0003` (register/login/logout/me; pending review).\n\n## Ecosystem Check — Candidate Building Blocks (verified 2026-08-18)\n\nAll four verification items below were run in a Claude session on 2026-08-18 using web search + doc fetch (the agent has web access now — the original \"user verifies current state\" premise no longer applies). This section keeps the shape of the question so it isn't re-litigated, with the verified state recorded per layer. The layer split still stands: the **MCP auth spec / `mcp` SDK** covers *client↔MCP-server* authentication (OAuth 2.1) and says nothing about *per-user, in-server* authorization — that second half is ours (ASR-002). Versions below are point-in-time snapshots; re-verify before scaffolding if a library has moved on. **Re-verified 2026-08-18 from source (tag v2.0.0) immediately before scaffolding — no drift;** the confirmed API surface is recorded in the Key Decisions row above.\n\nThe \"who's done the heavy lifting\" question resolves layer by layer:\n\n| Layer | What it does | Verified state (2026-08-18) |\n|-------|--------------|-----------------------------|\n| MCP client↔server auth | OAuth 2.1, token verification in the server | `mcp` Python SDK **2.0.0** (released 2026-07-28 — current stable; v1.x now security-patch-only): `mcp.server.auth` `TokenVerifier` + `AuthSettings` (FastMCP is now `MCPServer`). Server is an OAuth 2.1 *resource server*; the SDK has no opinion on what a valid token looks like — our verifier does. `stateless_http=True` for per-request statelessness |\n| Identity + JWT | self-issued JWT, user identity | `PyJWT 2.13.0` (active) — the pick. `fastapi-users 15.0.5` is **maintenance mode** (no new features; an unreleased successor toolkit is promised) — don't build new work on it. `authlib 1.7.2` (active) held in reserve only if the MCP server later needs its own interactive authorization server |\n| Token vault | store (user UUID → JWT) | unchanged: a POC-scale store behind an interface first (e.g. a Postgres table); `hvac 2.4.0` (active) is the escape hatch if a real HashiCorp Vault is wanted |\n| Per-user vault + capability resolver | the novel part — no off-the-shelf library known | re-confirmed ours to design (ASR-002: \"squarely this project's own design\") — verification found no off-the-shelf per-user token resolver for a multi-tenant MCP server |\n\nVerification list (all four completed 2026-08-18 in a Claude session with web access — results recorded below):\n\n1. `mcp` Python SDK: current auth capabilities; statelessness support; how per-request context (the user id) reaches tool handlers.\n2. **Native Claude API MCP connector**: exact auth mechanics for remote servers via the API — can it carry a per-user token, or does it do user-scoped OAuth on the user's behalf? This determines where user identity enters the system and may change the design.\n3. Anthropic published guidance on the MCP credential/vulnerability topic — anything naming the exposure paths (feeds the threat model step).\n4. Current state of `fastapi-users` / `authlib` for the identity half.\n\n**Prejudgment to test → verified 2026-08-18: confirmed, with one correction.** *Compose standard parts + one small custom component* holds — OAuth 2.1 verification on the MCP server comes from the SDK (`TokenVerifier`), identity from `PyJWT`, and the per-user vault + capability resolver is the one genuinely custom piece. The *other* branch of the prejudgment did **not** fire: the Claude API MCP connector requires the consumer to supply and refresh the token per call (item 2) — it manages nothing on the user's behalf — so the self-issued JWT + vault + resolver is **not** simplified away. It stays exactly as the 10k-foot design has it, with the resolver's output landing in `mcp_servers[].authorization_token` on each Messages API call.\n\n### Verification results (2026-08-18)\n\n**1. `mcp` Python SDK** — `mcp` **2.0.0** on PyPI (released 2026-07-28): v2 is the current stable line — a major rework built for the **2026-07-28 spec revision** (and every earlier one); v1.x continues on a frozen line receiving security patches only, documented at `py.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/v1/`. `FastMCP` is renamed `MCPServer` (import `mcp.server`); `get_context()` is gone — declare a `ctx: Context` parameter instead. **Statelessness: first-class** — the 2026-07-28 spec removes the connection handshake, the session, and every server-initiated request, and `mcp.run(transport=\"streamable-http\", stateless_http=True)` gives \"a fresh transport per request, no session tracking\" (plus `json_response=True`, `event_store`, and `transport_security` for DNS-rebinding protection when deployed off-localhost). **Auth: the resource-server half of OAuth 2.1** — implement `TokenVerifier` (one async method: raw token in → `AccessToken | None` out; the SDK has no opinion on what a valid token is) together with `AuthSettings(issuer_url, resource_server_url, required_scopes)`; the SDK auto-serves RFC 9728 protected-resource metadata at `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/...` and answers unauthenticated requests with a 401 whose `WWW-Authenticate` points at it; the repo ships an RFC 7662 `IntrospectionTokenVerifier` example as the production shape. The legacy embedded authorization server (`auth_server_provider=`) is explicitly not recommended for new servers. **How per-request context reaches handlers:** `get_access_token()` (`mcp.server.auth.middleware.auth_context`) returns the current request's `AccessToken` — `client_id`, `subject`, `scopes`, `expires_at`, plus any extra claims the verifier attached — inside any tool/resource/prompt handler via a per-request context variable; the user UUID rides in as a JWT claim decoded by *our* verifier, with zero plumbing. Caveat: auth exists only on the HTTP transports — over stdio or in memory it is always `None` (the stdio security boundary is the launching process). Sources: pypi.org/project/mcp; `py.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io` (What's new in v2, Running your server, Authorization); modelcontextprotocol/python-sdk.\n\n**2. Native Claude API MCP connector** — the Messages API \"MCP connector\" feature; current beta header `mcp-client-2025-11-20` (the 2025-04-04 header is deprecated). Available on Claude API, Claude Platform on AWS, and Microsoft Foundry (all beta). **Exact auth mechanics (the design-critical answer):** each `mcp_servers` entry takes `type: \"url\"` (must start with https), `name`, and an optional `authorization_token` — and the official docs state: \"API consumers are expected to handle the OAuth flow and obtain the access token prior to making the API call, and to refresh the token as needed.\" → **The connector carries a per-user token that *we* supply per call; it does NOT do user-scoped OAuth on the user's behalf in the API path.** (The claude.ai *product* connector is a different mechanism — consent-gated OAuth, `oauth_anthropic_creds`, no client_credentials — and is not this POC's target; ASR-003 targets the API.) Constraints that shape the POC: the server must be publicly exposed over HTTP (Streamable HTTP or SSE; no stdio); **only tool calls** travel through the connector (no prompts or resources); multiple servers per request is supported; per-tool allowlist / denylist / `defer_loading` live in `mcp_toolset` objects in the `tools` array; the Batches API accepts `mcp_servers` too; and **MCP-connector data is not covered by ZDR** (standard retention applies — note this when scoping what the tools can return). The in-SDK alternative for local/stdio servers — `anthropic[mcp]` client-side helpers (`anthropic.lib.tools.mcp`) — is ASR-003's \"hand-rolled client/loop in this app's own backend\" option, now a one-import path. Source: platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/mcp-connector; claude.com/docs/connectors/building/authentication.\n\n**3. Published security guidance (feeds the threat-model step)** — the canonical sources found: (a) the **MCP spec's \"Security Best Practices\"** document (2026-07-28 revision; modelcontextprotocol.io) — names, at MUST level: the confused deputy (proxy static client id + consent-cookie theft), **token passthrough (explicitly forbidden — \"MCP servers MUST NOT accept any tokens that were not explicitly issued for the MCP server\"; audience validation per RFC 9068)**, SSRF via OAuth discovery URLs (client side) and via CIMD (server side), **state-handle hijacking** (\"MCP is stateless and has no protocol-level sessions\" — servers MUST NOT treat possession of a state handle as authentication; bind handles server-side to the user id *derived from the verified token*), local MCP server compromise (malicious startup commands, DNS rebinding against localhost), OAuth authorization URL validation (javascript:/shell handling → XSS/RCE), stdio-proxy privilege escalation, mix-up attacks (RFC 9207), localhost redirect impersonation, CIMD trust policies, and progressive least-privilege scope minimization. (b) The **OWASP MCP Security Cheat Sheet** — the prompt-injection / tool-poisoning class plus the 2025 incident record (Asana cross-tenant data exposure, GitHub MCP prompt injection, unauthenticated RCE in Anthropic's own MCP Inspector, npm supply-chain compromises). (c) **Anthropic-specific:** no dedicated Anthropic blog post naming MCP credential exposure paths was found; the closest official guidance is the Claude Code security doc — prompt-injection safeguards (permission system, isolated context windows for web fetch, input sanitization, network-command approval), trust verification for new MCP servers, and the key precedent for this POC's core move: in Anthropic-hosted cloud sessions, \"authentication is handled through a secure proxy that uses a scoped credential inside the sandbox, which is then translated to your actual GitHub authentication token\" — i.e. *a user identifier enters the agent's world; the credential does not*. **Gap to close in the threat-model step:** neither source fully covers the POC's specific concern — a self-issued vault token echoed into model context via tool output, model-visible parameters, or logs. The vault + bare-user-id design is the defense for exactly that; name those paths explicitly in the concrete threat model.\n\n**4. Identity libraries (the \"batteries-included\" candidates)** — `fastapi-users` **15.0.5** (released 2026-03-27): **maintenance mode** — \"security updates and dependency maintenance, no new features\"; the maintainers are building \"a new Python authentication toolkit that will ultimately supersede FastAPI Users\" (unreleased). Feature set otherwise unchanged (register/login/reset/verify routes, social OAuth2, pluggable JWT/database/Redis strategies, SQLAlchemy-async/Beanie). `PyJWT` **2.13.0** (2026-05-21): active, production/stable, trusted publishing with sigstore attestation. `authlib` **1.7.2** (2026-05-06; signed releases; v1.7.0 landed 2026-04): active, full OAuth 2 + OpenID Connect client *and* server, joserfc-based, Python 3.10–3.14. `hvac` **2.4.0** (2025-10-30): active HashiCorp Vault API client, Apache-2.0. **POC selection:** `PyJWT` for the self-issued JWT half — smallest footprint, matches ASR-002's self-contained-JWT lean, and makes no bet on a superseded library; hold `authlib` in reserve if the MCP server ever needs its own interactive authorization server (e.g. for non-API clients); the vault stays a Postgres table behind an interface at POC scale, with `hvac` as the escape hatch if a real vault is wanted.\n\nTwo design consequences to carry into the open questions (both follow from items 1 + 2, not from the POC's own reasoning): (1) the self-issued JWT must be one the MCP server's `TokenVerifier` can validate — audience bound to the MCP server's endpoint, per the spec's token-passthrough rule (RFC 9068); and (2) the backend needs a token-lifecycle path — re-fetch or re-mint from the vault on 401 — because the connector consumes one token per call and the spec's stateless posture gives it nothing else. Also, when scaffolding: pin `mcp>=2.0,<3` (v2 is three weeks old at verification time) and read the migration guide before writing any v1-style (`FastMCP`) code.\n\n## Collaboration & Communication Style\n\n- **User background:** Experienced C# developer, new to Python.\n- **Motivation:** Adopting Python after the new (stateless) Anthropic MCP SDK — with no foreseeable C# support.\n- **Communication:** The user describes requirements and concepts in **C# terms**. Translate the intent, then express it idiomatically in Python.\n- **Do NOT** write \"C# in Python clothing\" — no `foreach`-style habits, verbose property boilerplate, or ported C# patterns. Follow Python best practices in all areas:\n  - PEP 8 style; `snake_case` for variables/functions/modules, `PascalCase` for classes, `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` for constants\n  - Dataclasses / `TypedDict` over C#-style classes for plain data; `Optional[T]`/`X | None` typing\n  - `pathlib.Path` over raw strings; `with` statements for resources\n  - Comprehensions and generator expressions where readable\n  - `black` formatting, `ruff` linting, `mypy` for type checking (or agreed equivalents)\n  - `pytest` for tests (matches the target project)\n- **Teaching moments:** When a Python idiom replaces a C# pattern the user mentioned, briefly note the correspondence (e.g., \"C# `IEnumerable<T>` + LINQ → Python generator + comprehension\") so the user can learn from the review. Keep notes short; don't over-explain basics an experienced developer doesn't need.\n- **Type hints on everything** public; this is the closest thing to C#'s static typing and helps the user's review.\n- **One exception to pure-Python naming:** the target codebase keeps the C# `I` prefix on interface names (`IUserProvider`, `IAuthorizationProvider`). Match that when using those names; everything else stays conventionally Pythonic.\n\n## Local LM Studio Agent — Role & Workflow\n\n- **Why:** Peak Claude sessions are budget-constrained (usage + provider costs already eat into it). A local model in LM Studio (currently `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` via MLX, with `mcp/filesystem` and — once its MCP config is stable — web search) absorbs legwork between peak sessions, so peak time isn't spent on things a local model can do.\n- **Division of labor:**\n  - **Local LM Studio agent:** repo exploration (`list_directory` / `search_files` / `read_text_file`), the \"Ecosystem Check → Verification list\" research above, drafting scaffolding/docs/code in this POC repo.\n  - **Claude (peak sessions):** design decisions, architecture calls, and the **review/approval gate** — nothing produced here moves into `ai-research-blog` without a Claude session reviewing it first.\n- **Recording results:** when the local agent finishes a verification item or makes a finding, log it in this file (the Key Decisions table, or directly in the relevant table/section it touches) with a date, so the next peak session picks up from there instead of re-deriving it. (Precedent set 2026-08-18: the ecosystem-check verification ran in a Claude session — web search / fetch MCP tools available — and its results were logged the same way.)\n- **Guardrail:** this repo (`poc/`) is the sandbox. Nothing here is \"done\" until a Claude session has reviewed it and deliberately moved it into `ai-research-blog` — the local agent's output is a draft/proposal, not a merge-ready artifact.\n- **Known capability gap (found 2026-08-18, first full scaffolding run) — closed same day.** The local agent's toolset (`mcp/filesystem` + a web-fetch tool + a JS-only sandbox with no shell/`--allow-run` access) had **no way to execute Python or run `pytest`.** It confirmed this itself mid-session (reasoned through and ruled out `run_javascript`/Deno as a path to a shell) and correctly flagged \"not executed yet\" as a caveat in its handoff — that honesty held. But it meant every test file it wrote was composed by reasoning alone, never run, and cross-file contracts it invented in the same sitting (e.g., a fake DB connection's method signature vs. the real driver's shape used elsewhere) could drift without it ever noticing. First run: 31 failed / 12 errors / 22 passed out of ~65 tests, all from this class of bug — see the Key Decisions row above.\n- **`poc-test-runner` MCP tool (added 2026-08-18):** closes the gap above. Two tools, both hardcoded to this repo only — no parameter can point them anywhere else (path-traversal / absolute-path / `~` arguments are rejected before they ever reach a subprocess; source at `~/.lmstudio/tools/poc_test_runner.py`):\n  - `sync_deps()` — runs `uv sync --extra dev` in this repo.\n  - `run_tests(pytest_args=\"\")` — runs `uv run pytest {pytest_args}` in this repo, returns stdout/stderr/returncode. Empty `pytest_args` runs the whole suite; e.g. `\"-k vault\"` or `\"tests/test_vault.py -v\"` scopes it.\n  - **Use it whenever you write or change any code in `src/` or `tests/`: call `sync_deps` once if dependencies might be stale, then `run_tests` after every change, and keep iterating until it's green — or until the tool stops you (see Blocked-Task Protocol below) — before handing back to a peak session.** Don't hand off code you haven't run through this tool — that was exactly the gap that produced the 2026-08-18 failures above.\n  - **`run_tests` has a per-session budget (12 calls / 45 minutes, added 2026-08-18)**, enforced by the tool itself, not by your judgment — it starts warning at call 9 and stops running pytest entirely past call 12 or 45 minutes, returning a message pointing back here. This exists because nothing else bounds how long a fix-and-rerun loop can run once a failure turns out to be genuinely hard, and a long session is exactly where prose instructions like \"stop if you're stuck\" tend to get lost. Budget it accordingly: don't burn 5 calls re-running the whole suite to check one line of a fix — use `pytest_args` to scope to just the failing test(s) while iterating, and only run the full suite once you think you're done or once per few fixes.\n\n  ### Operating discipline (added 2026-08-19, after a session read SDK source for 1500+ lines instead of ever calling the tool)\n\n  The 2026-08-18 capability gap (no way to execute Python) is closed — `poc-test-runner` exists and is reachable. The failure mode now is behavioral, not a missing capability, so it needs a behavioral rule, not another tool:\n\n  - **First tool call, no exceptions:** in any session touching `src/` or `tests/`, the first tool call is `sync_deps` (if dependencies might be stale) followed by a full-suite `run_tests` — before reading any source file, library or otherwise. Get the real, current failure list before doing anything else. Planning to call it later in your own reasoning does not count as having called it — call it first, literally.\n  - **Read library/SDK source only when a specific failing test's traceback names it.** You have an actual error in hand and need to understand the one function/class it points at — that's the only license to open a third-party source file. Never read SDK internals to pre-emptively reason out how a feature \"probably\" behaves; run the test and read what actually happened instead of simulating it in your head.\n  - **Stay inside the assigned scope.** Fix the root causes named in the current task, or whatever `run_tests` actually reports as failing — nothing else. If something else looks interesting while you're in a file, don't chase it: note it in this file under Key Decisions for a peak session to triage later.\n  - **If a tool you expect (`run_tests`, `sync_deps`) isn't in your available tool list this session, say so explicitly in your handoff and stop** — that's a session/config problem for the user to fix, not something to work around by reasoning blind.\n\n  ### Blocked-Task Protocol\n  Triggered when `run_tests` refuses to run (budget exceeded), or on your own judgment if a specific failure has survived 2-3 real fix attempts. Stop touching that test. In this file, under the relevant Key Decisions or Current State entry, record for each test still failing:\n  - **Test name** (e.g. `tests/test_vault.py::test_put_upserts`)\n  - **Exact error/traceback** from your last `run_tests` call — not a paraphrase\n  - **What you tried** — each attempt and why it didn't work\n  - **Your best hypothesis** for the remaining cause, even if unproven\n\n  Then move on: fix what you can elsewhere in the suite, and leave the rest for peak-session review. A partial fix with an honest, detailed write-up on the remainder is far more useful than a full session spent on one stubborn test with nothing to show for it.\n\n## Conventions\n\n- Language: Python 3.12+ (the POC requires `>=3.12`; the target repo runs 3.13.7 — compatible)\n- Styling: `black` + `ruff` (line length 100); types enforced with `mypy` (config in pyproject; untyped third-party deps overridden there)\n- Tests: `pytest`, in `tests/` mirroring source layout. DB-dependent components are tested against an in-memory fake connection via the injectable `connect` parameter (the target repo's pattern) — the suite needs zero infrastructure; `test_integration.py` covers the real-Postgres path and skips unless the POC env vars are set\n- Env/secrets: `.env` (gitignored) + committed `.env.example` with placeholders only — same pattern as the target repo; **never a real secret in a committed file**. Entry points (not library modules) call `load_dotenv()` — the target repo's no-import-time-side-effects discipline\n- Package layout: one flat `src/mcpauth` package (hatchling, src layout) — held loose until the deployment topology is confirmed (docs/0001); module boundaries kept extraction-clean so the port to `ai-research-blog` is a move\n- Naming: the target repo's `I` prefix applies to `IUserProvider` / `IAuthorizationProvider`; everything else conventionally Pythonic (no interface for a single implementation — the vault and identity anchor are concrete on purpose)\n\n## Current State & Next Steps\n\n- [x] Set project context (this file): goal, 10k-foot design, integration target\n- [x] Align with `ai-research-blog` docs (ASR-002/003, ADR-0005)\n- [x] Verify current ecosystem state (see Ecosystem Check) — done 2026-08-18 in a Claude session with web access\n- [x] Concrete threat model — done 2026-08-18, see Threat Model section\n- [x] Decide JWT shape — self-contained, committed 2026-08-18\n- [x] Decide vault implementation at POC scale — Postgres table, committed 2026-08-18\n- [x] Define the identity anchor shape — fixed anchor + triples, committed 2026-08-18\n- [ ] Confirm deployment topology — decision brief in docs/0001 (A/B/C + the tied signing-algorithm decision); recommendation logged 2026-08-18 (in-process resolver); needs user sign-off, tied to MCP-server placement\n- [ ] Enumerate the actual roles/claims list (owner, subscriber, admin?) — the shape is decided, the list isn't; drafted in docs/0002 (pending review)\n- [ ] Define the security app's API contract (what the resolver process exposes) — drafted in docs/0003 (pending review)\n- [x] Set up project scaffolding — done 2026-08-18: pyproject + `src/mcpauth` (single flat package — layout held loose until the deployment topology is confirmed) + 12 test files + docs 0001–0003 + scripts; suite runs on an in-memory fake connection (zero infra), real-Postgres tests skip without the env vars. **Draft — pending review** per the guardrail above\n- [x] **Review the scaffolding** (peak Claude, 2026-08-18): reviewed and **not** approved as-is — see the Key Decisions row above. The docs/0001–0003 design content and AGENTS.md updates the local agent produced are sound and stay; the code+tests need a fix pass before anything moves to `ai-research-blog`\n- [ ] **NEXT TASK for the local LM Studio agent (reissued 2026-08-19 — the 2026-08-18 attempt at this task churned on SDK source for a full session without ever calling the tool; see the 2026-08-19 Key Decisions row and \"Operating discipline\" above. Task itself is unchanged):** fix the test suite, using the `poc-test-runner` MCP tool (`sync_deps` then `run_tests` — see \"Local LM Studio Agent\" section above) to actually verify every fix instead of reasoning blind. **Your first tool call this session must be `sync_deps` then `run_tests`, before reading anything else.** Last known baseline: 31 failed / 12 errors / 22 passed / 1 skipped. At minimum, three known root causes to fix: (a) `tests/conftest.py`'s `_FakeConnection.cursor()` doesn't match real `psycopg2`'s two-step `cursor()` → `cur.execute(sql, params)` shape that `src/mcpauth` actually uses; (b) `test_auth_app.py` builds a sync `httpx.Client` over `ASGITransport`, which is async-only — switch to `httpx.AsyncClient` + `pytest-asyncio` (or a sync `TestClient`); (c) `test_resolver.py` references a `_logged_in_user` fixture that doesn't exist in `conftest.py` — add it or fix whatever the test meant to reference. This can be used to provide a human-readable\n    identifier for the root, which may be useful for display purposes or for\n    referencing the root in other parts of the application.\n    \"\"\"\n    uri: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The URI identifying the root. This *must* start with `file://` for now.\n    This restriction may be relaxed in future versions of the protocol to allow\n    other URI schemes.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass Prompts(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Present if the server offers any prompt templates.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    list_changed: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"listChanged\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Whether this server supports notifications for changes to the prompt list.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass Resources(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Present if the server offers any resources to read.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    list_changed: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"listChanged\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Whether this server supports notifications for changes to the resource list.\n    \"\"\"\n    subscribe: bool | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Whether this server supports subscribing to resource updates.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass Tools(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Present if the server offers any tools to call.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    list_changed: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"listChanged\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Whether this server supports notifications for changes to the tool list.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass StringSchema(WireModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    default: str | None = None\n    description: str | None = None\n    format: Literal[\"date\", \"date-time\", \"email\", \"uri\"] | None = None\n    max_length: Annotated[int | None, Field(alias=\"maxLength\")] = None\n    min_length: Annotated[int | None, Field(alias=\"minLength\")] = None\n    title: str | None = None\n    type: Literal[\"string\"]\n\n\nclass SubscriptionFilter(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    The set of notification types a client may opt in to on a\n    {@link SubscriptionsListenRequestsubscriptions/listen} request.\n\n    Each notification type is **opt-in**; the server **MUST NOT** send\n    notification types the client has not explicitly requested here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    prompts_list_changed: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"promptsListChanged\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    If true, receive {@link PromptListChangedNotificationnotifications/prompts/list_changed}.\n    \"\"\"\n    resource_subscriptions: Annotated[list[str] | None, Field(alias=\"resourceSubscriptions\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Subscribe to {@link ResourceUpdatedNotificationnotifications/resources/updated} for these resource URIs.\n    Replaces the former `resources/subscribe` RPC.\n    \"\"\"\n    resources_list_changed: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"resourcesListChanged\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    If true, receive {@link ResourceListChangedNotificationnotifications/resources/list_changed}.\n    \"\"\"\n    tools_list_changed: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"toolsListChanged\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    If true, receive {@link ToolListChangedNotificationnotifications/tools/list_changed}.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass SubscriptionsListenResultMeta(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Extends {@link ResultMetaObject} with the subscription-stream identifier carried by a\n    {@link SubscriptionsListenResult}. All key naming rules from `MetaObject` apply.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"allow\",\n    )\n    io_modelcontextprotocol_server_info: Annotated[Any | None, Field(alias=\"io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Identifies the server software producing the response. Servers SHOULD\n    include this field on every response unless specifically configured not\n    to do so.\n\n    The {@link Implementation} schema requires `name` and `version`; other\n    fields are optional.\n\n    The value is self-reported by the server and is not verified by the\n    protocol. It is intended for display, logging, and debugging. Clients\n    SHOULD NOT use it to change their behavior, and SHOULD NOT rely on it for\n    security decisions.\n    \"\"\"\n    io_modelcontextprotocol_subscription_id: Annotated[RequestId, Field(alias=\"io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId\")]\n    \"\"\"\n    Identifies the subscription stream this response closes, so the client can\n    correlate it with the originating subscription — mirroring the same key on\n    the stream's notifications. The value is the JSON-RPC ID of the\n    `subscriptions/listen` request that opened the stream (and equals this\n    response's `id`).\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass TextResourceContents(WireModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[MetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    mime_type: Annotated[str | None, Field(alias=\"mimeType\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    The MIME type of this resource, if known.\n    \"\"\"\n    text: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The text of the item. This must only be set if the item can actually be represented as text (not binary data).\n    \"\"\"\n    uri: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The URI of this resource.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass AnyOfItem(WireModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    const: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The constant enum value.\n    \"\"\"\n    title: str\n    \"\"\"\n    Display title for this option.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass Items(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Schema for array items with enum options and display labels.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n TitledMultiSelectEnumSchema(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Schema for multiple-selection enumeration with display titles for each option.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n enum field.\n    \"\"\"\n    items: Items\n    \"\"\"\n    Schema for array items with enum options and display labels.\n    \"\"\"\n    max_items: Annotated[int | None, Field(alias=\"maxItems\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Maximum number of items to select.\n    \"\"\"\n    min_items: Annotated[int | None, Field(alias=\"minItems\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Minimum number of items to select.\n    \"\"\"\n    title: str | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional title for the enum field.\n    \"\"\"\n    type: Literal[\"array\"]\n\n\nclass OneOfItem(WireModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    const: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The enum value.\n    \"\"\"\n    title: str\n    \"\"\"\n    Display label for this option.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass TitledSingleSelectEnumSchema(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Schema for single-selection enumeration with display titles for each option.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    default: str | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional default value.\n    \"\"\"\n    description: str | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional description for the enum field.\n    \"\"\"\n    one_of: Annotated[list[OneOfItem], Field(alias=\"oneOf\")]\n    \"\"\"\n    Array of enum options with values and display labels.\n    \"\"\"\n    title: str | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional title for the enum field.\n    \"\"\"\n    type: Literal[\"string\"]\n\n\nclass InputSchema(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    A JSON Schema object defining the expected parameters for the tool.\n\n    Tool arguments are always JSON objects, so `type: \"object\"` is required at the root.\n    Beyond that, any JSON Schema 2020-12 keyword may appear alongside `type` — including\n    composition keywords (`oneOf`, `anyOf`, `allOf`, `not`), conditional keywords\n    (`if`/`then`/`else`), reference keywords (`$ref`, `$defs`, `$anchor`), and any other\n    standard validation or annotation keywords.\n\n    Property schemas may carry an `x-mcp-header` annotation to mirror the\n    argument value into an HTTP header on the Streamable HTTP transport. See\n    the Streamable HTTP transport specification for the validity and\n    extraction rules.\n\n    Defaults to JSON Schema 2020-12 when no explicit `$schema` is provided.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"allow\",\n    )\n    schema_: Annotated[str | None, Field(alias=\"$schema\")] = None\n    type: Literal[\"object\"]\n\n\nclass OutputSchema(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    An optional JSON Schema object defining the structure of the tool's output returned in\n    the structuredContent field of a {@link CallToolResult}. This can be any valid JSON Schema 2020-12.\n\n    Defaults to JSON Schema 2020-12 when no explicit `$schema` is provided.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"allow\",\n    )\n    schema_: Annotated[str | None, Field(alias=\"$schema\")] = None\n\n\nclass ToolAnnotations(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Additional properties describing a {@link Tool} to clients.\n\n    NOTE: all properties in `ToolAnnotations` are **hints**.\n    They are not guaranteed to provide a faithful description of\n    tool behavior (including descriptive properties like `title`).\n\n    Clients should never make tool use decisions based on `ToolAnnotations`\n    received from untrusted servers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    destructive_hint: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"destructiveHint\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    If true, the tool may perform destructive updates to its environment.\n    If false, the tool performs only additive updates.\n\n    (This property is meaningful only when `readOnlyHint == false`)\n\n    Default: true\n    \"\"\"\n    idempotent_hint: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"idempotentHint\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    If true, calling the tool repeatedly with the same arguments\n    will have no additional effect on its environment.\n\n    (This property is meaningful only when `readOnlyHint == false`)\n\n    Default: false\n    \"\"\"\n    open_world_hint: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"openWorldHint\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    If true, this tool may interact with an \"open world\" of external\n    entities. If false, the tool's domain of interaction is closed.\n    For example, the world of a web search tool is open, whereas that\n    of a memory tool is not.\n\n    Default: true\n    \"\"\"\n    read_only_hint: Annotated[bool | None, Field(alias=\"readOnlyHint\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    If true, the tool does not modify its environment.\n\n    Default: false\n    \"\"\"\n    title: str | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    A human-readable title for the tool.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass ToolChoice(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Controls tool selection behavior for sampling requests.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    mode: Literal[\"auto\", \"none\", \"required\"] | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Controls the tool use ability of the model:\n    - `\"auto\"`: Model decides whether to use tools (default)\n    - `\"required\"`: Model MUST use at least one tool before completing\n    - `\"none\"`: Model MUST NOT use any tools\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass ToolUseContent(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    A request from the assistant to call a tool.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[MetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional metadata about the tool use. Clients SHOULD preserve this field when\n    including tool uses in subsequent sampling requests to enable caching optimizations.\n    \"\"\"\n    id: str\n    \"\"\"\n    A unique identifier for this tool use.\n\n    This ID is used to match tool results to their corresponding tool uses.\n    \"\"\"\n    input: dict[str, Any]\n    \"\"\"\n    The arguments to pass to the tool, conforming to the tool's input schema.\n    \"\"\"\n    name: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The name of the tool to call.\n    \"\"\"\n    type: Literal[\"tool_use\"]\n\n\nclass Data1(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Additional information about the error. The value of this member is defined by the sender (e.g. detailed error information, nested errors etc.).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    requested: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The protocol version that was requested by the client.\n    \"\"\"\n    supported: list[str]\n    \"\"\"\n    Protocol versions the server supports. The client should choose a\n    mutually supported version from this list and retry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass Error3(Error):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    code: Literal[-32022]\n    \"\"\"\n    The error type that occurred.\n    \"\"\"\n    data: Data1\n    \"\"\"\n    Additional information about the error. The value of this member is defined by the sender (e.g. detailed error information, nested errors etc.).\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass UnsupportedProtocolVersionError(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Returned when the request's protocol version is unknown to the server or\n    unsupported (e.g., a known experimental or draft version the server has\n    chosen not to implement). For HTTP, the response status code MUST be\n    `400 Bad Request`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    error: Error3\n    id: RequestId | None = None\n    jsonrpc: Literal[\"2.0\"]\n\n\nclass Items1(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Schema for the array items.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    enum: list[str]\n    \"\"\"\n    Array of enum values to choose from.\n    \"\"\"\n    type: Literal[\"string\"]\n\n\nclass UntitledMultiSelectEnumSchema(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Schema for items.\n    \"\"\"\n    max_items: Annotated[int | None, Field(alias=\"maxItems\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Maximum number of items to select.\n    \"\"\"\n    min_items: Annotated[int | None, Field(alias=\"minItems\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Minimum number of items to select.\n    \"\"\"\n    title: str | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional title for the enum field.\n    \"\"\"\n title for the enum field.\n    \"\"\"\n    type: Literal[\"string\"]\n\n\nclass Annotations(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional annotations for the client. The client can use annotations to inform how objects are used or displayed\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    audience: list[Role] | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Describes who the intended audience of this object or data is.\n\n    It can include multiple entries to indicate content useful for multiple audiences (e.g., `[\"user\", \"assistant\"]`).\n    \"\"\"\n    last_modified: Annotated[str | None, Field(alias=\"lastModified\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    The moment the resource was last modified, as an ISO 8601 formatted string.\n\n    Should be an ISO 8601 formatted string (e.g., \"2025-01-12T15:00:58Z\").\n\n    Examples: last activity timestamp in an open file, timestamp when the resource\n    was attached, etc.\n    \"\"\"\n    priority: Annotated[float | None, Field(ge=0.0, le=1.0)] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Describes how important this data is for operating the server.\n\n    A value of 1 means \"most important,\" and indicates that the data is\n    effectively required, while 0 means \"least important,\" and indicates that\n    the data is entirely optional.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass AudioContent(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Audio provided to or from an LLM.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[MetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    annotations: Annotations | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional annotations for the client.\n    \"\"\"\n    data: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The base64-encoded audio data.\n    \"\"\"\n    mime_type: Annotated[str, Field(alias=\"mimeType\")]\n    \"\"\"\n    The MIME type of the audio. Different providers may support different audio types.\n    \"\"\"\n    type: Literal[\"audio\"]\n\n\nclass BlobResourceContents(WireModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[MetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    blob: str\n    \"\"\"\n    A base64-encoded string representing the binary data of the item.\n    \"\"\"\n    mime_type: Annotated[str | None, Field(alias=\"mimeType\")] = None\n    \"\"\"\n    The MIME type of this resource, if known.\n    \"\"\"\n    uri: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The URI of this resource.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass CacheableResult(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    A result that supports a time-to-live (TTL) hint for client-side caching.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[ResultMetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    cache_scope: Annotated[Literal[\"private\", \"public\"], Field(alias=\"cacheScope\")]\n    \"\"\"\n    Indicates the intended scope of the cached response, analogous to HTTP\n    `Cache-Control: public` vs `Cache-Control: private`.\n\n    - `\"public\"`: The response does not contain user-specific data. Any\n      client or intermediary (e.g., shared gateway, caching proxy) MAY cache\n      the response and serve it across authorization contexts.\n    - `\"private\"`: The response MAY be cached and reused only within the\n      same authorization context. Caches MUST NOT be shared across\n      authorization contexts (e.g., a different access token requires a\n      different cache).\n    \"\"\"\n    result_type: Annotated[str, Field(alias=\"resultType\")]\n    \"\"\"\n    Indicates the type of the result, which allows the client to determine\n    how to parse the result object.\n\n    Servers implementing this protocol version MUST include this field.\n    For backward compatibility, when a client receives a result from a\n    server implementing an earlier protocol version (which does not include\n    `resultType`), the client MUST treat the absent field as `\"complete\"`.\n    \"\"\"\n    ttl_ms: Annotated[int, Field(alias=\"ttlMs\", ge=0)]\n    \"\"\"\n    A hint from the server indicating how long (in milliseconds) the\n    client MAY cache this response before re-fetching. Semantics are\n    analogous to HTTP Cache-Control max-age.\n\n    - If 0, The response SHOULD be considered immediately stale,\n      The client MAY re-fetch every time the result is needed.\n    - If positive, the client SHOULD consider the result fresh for this many\n      milliseconds after receiving the response.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass CompleteResult(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    The result returned by the server for a {@link CompleteRequestcompletion/complete} request.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[ResultMetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    completion: Completion\n    result_type: Annotated[str, Field(alias=\"resultType\")]\n    \"\"\"\n    Indicates the type of the result, which allows the client to determine\n    how to parse the result object.\n\n    Servers implementing this protocol version MUST include this field.\n    For backward compatibility, when a client receives a result from a\n    server implementing an earlier protocol version (which does not include\n    `resultType`), the client MUST treat the absent field as `\"complete\"`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n\nclass CompleteResultResponse(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    A successful response from the server for a {@link CompleteRequestcompletion/complete} request.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    id: RequestId\n    jsonrpc: Literal[\"2.0\"]\n    result: CompleteResult\n\n\nclass ElicitRequestParams(RootModel[ElicitRequestFormParams | ElicitRequestURLParams]):\n EmbeddedResource(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    The contents of a resource, embedded into a prompt or tool call result.\n\n    It is up to the client how best to render embedded resources for the benefit\n    of the LLM and/or the user.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[MetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    annotations: Annotations | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional annotations for the client.\n    \"\"\"\n    resource: TextResourceContents | BlobResourceContents\n    type: Literal[\"resource\"]\n\n\nclass EnumSchema(\n    RootModel[\n        UntitledSingleSelectEnumSchema\n        | TitledSingleSelectEnumSchema\n        | UntitledMultiSelectEnumSchema\n        | UntitledMultiSelectEnumSchema\n        | TitledMultiSelectEnumSchema\n        | LegacyTitledEnumSchema\n    )\n\n\nclass HeaderMismatchError(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Returned when a server rejects a request because the values in the HTTP\n    headers do not match the corresponding values in the request body, or\n    because required headers are missing or malformed. For HTTP, the response\n    status code MUST be `400 Bad Request`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    error: Error1\n    id: RequestId | None = None\n    jsonrpc: Literal[\"2.0\"]\n\n\nclass ImageContent(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    An image provided to or from an LLM.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[MetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n    annotations: Annotations | None = None\n    \"\"\"\n    Optional annotations for the client.\n    \"\"\"\n    data: str\n    \"\"\"\n    The base64-encoded image data.\n    \"\"\"\n    mime_type: Annotated[str, Field(alias=\"mimeType\")]\n    \"\"\"\n    The MIME type of the image. Different providers may support different image types.\n    \"\"\"\n    type: Literal[\"image\"]\n\n\nclass JSONRPCErrorResponse(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    A response to a request that indicates an error occurred.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    error: Error\n    id: RequestId | None = None\n    jsonrpc: Literal[\"2.0\"]\n\n\nclass JSONRPCRequest(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    A request that expects a response.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    id: RequestId\n    jsonrpc: Literal[\"2.0\"]\n    method: str\n    params: dict[str, Any] | None = None\n\n\nclass Params(WireModel):\n    model_config = ConfigDict(\n        extra=\"ignore\",\n    )\n    meta: Annotated[MetaObject | None, Field(alias=\"_meta\")] = None\n\n\nclass ListRootsRequest(WireModel):\n    \"\"\"\n    Sent from the server to request a list of root URIs from the client.
